Research and implement obsidian skills so we can better leverage our vault according to best practices

Base directory for this skill: /home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/.claude/skills/vault-health

# Vault Health Audit

Run a comprehensive health check on the Obsidian vault, surfacing issues that degrade navigation, discoverability, and knowledge graph density.

## When to Use

- Periodic vault maintenance (weekly/monthly)
- After bulk imports or automated linking runs
- Before presentations or knowledge sharing
- When the graph view looks sparse or disconnected

## Execution Steps

### 1. Broken Wiki-Links

Find links pointing to non-existent notes:

```bash
# Extract all wiki-links, check which targets don't exist as files
grep -roh '\[\[[^]]*\]\]' concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ 2>/dev/null \
  | sed 's/\[\[//;s/\]\]//' | sort -u | while read link; do
  # Handle display text: [[target|display]] -> target
  target="${link%%|*}"
  fname="${target}.md"
  found=0
  for d in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments projects inbox daily; do
    [ -f "$d/$fname" ] && found=1 && break
  done
  [ $found -eq 0 ] && echo "$link"
done
```

Report: total count, top 10 examples, which directories have the most broken links.

### 2. Orphan Notes (No Inbound Links)

Find notes that no other note links to:

```bash
# For each .md file, check if any other file links to it
for dir in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments projects; do
  for f in "$dir"/*.md; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    basename="${f##*/}"
    name="${basename%.md}"
    # Count inbound links (excluding self-references)
    count=$(grep -rl "\[\[$name" concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ 2>/dev/null | grep -v "$f" | wc -l)
    [ "$count" -eq 0 ] && echo "ORPHAN: $f"
  done
done
```

Report: total orphan count by directory, list them.

### 3. Stub Notes

Find auto-generated stubs that need content:

```bash
grep -rl 'Auto-generated stub\|Add.*definition.*here\|\[Add.*here\]' concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ 2>/dev/null
```

Report: total count, list by directory.

### 4. Frontmatter Issues

Check for common frontmatter problems:

```bash
# Missing frontmatter entirely
for dir in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments projects; do
  for f in "$dir"/*.md; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    head -1 "$f" | grep -q '^---' || echo "NO_FRONTMATTER: $f"
  done
done

# Tags as string instead of array
grep -rl '^tags:' concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ 2>/dev/null | while read f; do
  grep '^tags:' "$f" | grep -qv '\[' && echo "TAGS_NOT_ARRAY: $f"
done

# Missing required fields per directory
# decisions/ needs: title, date, status, tags
# concepts/ needs: title, date, tags
```

### 5. Large Files

Check for files exceeding reasonable size:

```bash
find concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ -name '*.md' -size +50k -exec ls -lh {} \;
```

### 6. Summary Report

Present results as a structured report:

```markdown
## Vault Health Report — YYYY-MM-DD

| Metric | Count | Status |
|--------|-------|--------|
| Total notes | X | — |
| Broken wiki-links | X | 🔴/🟡/🟢 |
| Orphan notes | X | 🔴/🟡/🟢 |
| Stub notes | X | 🟡 |
| Frontmatter issues | X | 🔴/🟡/🟢 |
| Oversized files | X | 🟡 |
| Wiki-link density | X links / note avg | — |

### Recommended Actions
1. [Highest priority fix]
2. [Second priority]
3. [Third priority]

Use `/link` to add missing connections.
Use `/flesh-out` to expand stub notes.
Use `/triage` to process inbox items.
```

## Thresholds

| Metric | Green | Yellow | Red |
|--------|-------|--------|-----|
| Broken links | < 10 | 10-50 | > 50 |
| Orphan notes | < 5% | 5-15% | > 15% |
| Stubs | < 10 | 10-30 | > 30 |
| Frontmatter issues | 0 | 1-10 | > 10 |

## Notes

- This skill is read-only — it reports issues but does not fix them
- Use other skills (`/link`, `/flesh-out`, `/triage`) to act on findings
- Run after bulk operations to verify vault integrity
- Results can be saved to `daily/` as a maintenance log

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Obsidian vault knowledge management with wiki-links (`[[note-name]]`)
   - Bidirectional linking — every outbound link must have a corresponding backlink in the target note
   - YAML frontmatter with tags as arrays (`tags: [tag1, tag2]`), not multiline YAML sequences
   - Obsidian link resolution: "shortest path" matching means `[[lesson-05]]` resolves to `lessons/lesson-05.md` without needing a path prefix
   - Path-prefixed links (`[[concepts/foo]]`) vs bare links (`[[foo]]`) — Obsidian handles both but vault convention is bare
   - Knowledge graph density measured by: orphan notes (0 inbound), isolated notes (≤7 total connections), average links/note
   - Atomic note structure: concepts (Definition, Key Properties, Examples, Related, Relevance to Cohezion), decisions (Context, Decision, Consequences, Alternatives), patterns (Problem, Solution, Code Example, When to Use)

   **Frontmatter Fixes (24 files):**
   - 22 decision files had multiline YAML tags converted to inline arrays using a Python script:
     ```python
     def convert_tags_to_inline(filepath):
         # Matches "tags:\n- tag1\n- tag2\n" and converts to "tags: [tag1, tag2]\n"
         tags_match = re.search(r'^tags:\n((?:- .+\n)+)', frontmatter, re.MULTILINE)
         tag_lines = tags_match.group(1).strip().split('\n')
         tags = [line.lstrip('- ').strip().strip("'\"") for line in tag_lines]
         inline_tags = 'tags: [' + ', '.join(tags) + ']\n'
     ```

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **False orphan count after agent linking**: Initial re-check showed 25 "still orphaned" notes. The agents had used path-prefixed wiki-links like `[[decisions/2026-02-11-vault-first...]]` but the orphan-check grep used `\[\[$name` which only matches bare links. Fixed by using broader matching pattern `\[\[.*$name` that catches both formats. Result: 0 actual orphans.
   - **False stub count**: After all stubs were expanded, grep still found 2 "stubs" (`concept-testing.md`, `concept-optimization.md`). Investigation showed these contained the phrases "Auto-generated stub" and "[Add definition here]" as *descriptions of what stubs are*, not as actual placeholder text. These were false positives — all 33 stubs were genuinely expanded.
   - **Broken link count discrepancy (225 vs 127)**: Initial audit only searched 6 core directories. After discovering `lessons/`, `Agents/`, and other directories existed, re-running the check with all vault directories reduced broken links from 225 to 127 — many links resolved to files in `lessons/`, `learnings/`, etc.
   - **Duplicate lesson files**: `lessons/` at root AND `patterns/lessons/` contained identical files. Obsidian's shortest-path resolution means `[[lesson-11-team-agent-efficiency]]` correctly resolves to the root `lessons/` copy, so these weren't actually broken.
   - **Grep with spaces in filenames**: `grep: patterns/sheetsbr: No such file or directory` and `grep: idge-mcp-testing.md: No such file or directory` — a filename with spaces was being split by the shell. This was cosmetic and didn't affect results.

   - **Vault health audit methodology**: Built comprehensive bash-based checks for broken links, orphans, stubs, frontmatter, and oversized files. Learned that Obsidian resolves links by shortest unique path, so path-prefixed links aren't broken in Obsidian but are non-standard.

### Method

1. **Tag-based discovery**: Found notes sharing tags but lacking connections using a Python script that built a tag index and identified missing pairs
2. **Score-based prioritization**: Scored missing connections by cross-directory bonus (3 points) and shared tag count, prioritizing the highest-value connections
3. **Bidirectional enforcement**: Every outbound link was matched with a corresponding backlink in the target note
4. **Annotations**: Each link includes a brief annotation explaining the relationship (e.g., "M87*'s ergosphere, where the jet is launched, is the same frame-dragging regime that powers gravitational wave emission")
5. **Existing structure preservation**: Links were added to existing "Related" sections without disrupting note structure</result>

   ```python
   def convert_tags_to_inline(filepath):
       tags_match = re.search(r'^tags:\n((?:- .+\n)+)', frontmatter, re.MULTILINE)
       tag_lines = tags_match.group(1).strip().split('\n')
       tags = [line.lstrip('- ').strip().strip("'\"") for line in tag_lines]
       inline_tags = 'tags: [' + ', '.join(tags) + ']\n'
   ```

   **Vault Health Check Commands (core audit pattern used across all rounds):**
   ```bash
   # Broken links
   grep -roh '\[\[[^]]*\]\]' concepts/ papers/ decisions/ ... | sed 's/\[\[//;s/\]\]//' | sort -u | while read link; do
     target="${link%%|*}"; fname="${target}.md"; found=0
     for d in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments projects inbox daily; do
       [ -f "$d/$fname" ] && found=1 && break
     done
     [ $found -eq 0 ] && echo "$link"
   done

   # Orphan check
   for f in "$dir"/*.md; do
     name="${basename%.md}"
     count=$(grep -rl "\[\[.*$name" concepts/ papers/ ... | grep -v "$f" | wc -l)
     [ "$count" -eq 0 ] && echo "ORPHAN: $f"
   done

   # Thin notes
   size=$(sed '1,/^---$/{ /^---$/!d; d }' "$f" 2>/dev/null | wc -c)
   [ "$size" -lt 800 ] && echo "$size $f"
   ```

### Key Design Decisions

1. **Used bare `[[filename]]` wiki-links only** -- no directory prefixes, no backtick code references.
2. **The single `[[walkthrough]]` link connects all 69 walkthrough.md instances** across the Agents directory, since Obsidian resolves to all files with that name.
3. **Some links appear in multiple hub notes** where thematically appropriate (e.g., `[[readiness_report]]` in both `universe-simulation.md` and `anthropic-research-engineer.md`), which adds redundancy to the graph.
4. **Replaced backtick path references with wiki-links** rather than simply appending, keeping the Agent Outputs sections clean and consistent with the wiki-link format used elsewhere in the vault.</result>

### What was added per note type

**Decisions (ADR format):** Each stub decision that had empty `## Context`, `## Decision`, `## Chosen Option` sections was expanded with:
- **Context** -- why the decision was needed, what problem it solved
- **Decision** -- what was chosen, key design choices
- **Consequences** -- positive and negative outcomes
- **Alternatives Considered** -- rejected options with reasoning
- **Outbound wiki-links** -- connections to related concepts, patterns, and other decisions

**Patterns:** Each stub pattern that had empty `## Problem`, `## Solution`, `## Code Example`, `## When to Use` sections was expanded with:
- **Problem** -- what pain point the pattern addresses
- **Solution** -- how the pattern works, with step-by-step structure
- **Code Example** -- concrete code showing the pattern in action
- **When to Use / When Not** -- applicability guidance
- **Related Patterns / Decisions / Concepts** -- structured outbound wiki-links

Base directory for this skill: /home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/.claude/skills/vault-keeper

# Vault Keeper

Autonomous maintenance agent that keeps the Obsidian vault healthy, dense, navigable, and agent-readable. Orchestrates the five specialist skills (vault-health, link, flesh-out, triage, note) in a single prioritized workflow.

**This skill is PROACTIVE.** It does not wait to be invoked — it monitors vault health continuously and acts when issues are detected.

## Usage

```
/vault-keeper                    # Full maintenance cycle (audit → heal → report)
/vault-keeper --quick            # Quick health check only (read-only, 30 seconds)
/vault-keeper --triage           # Process inbox only
/vault-keeper --densify          # Link + flesh-out cycle only
/vault-keeper --moc              # Generate/update Maps of Content
/vault-keeper --context "query"  # Load relevant vault context for a task
```

---

## Proactive Behavior

The Vault Keeper monitors vault health automatically and acts without waiting for explicit user invocation. This is the **default behavior** — the keeper is always watching.

### Trigger: Session Start

At the **start of every session** in the cohezion-vault:

**Step 1: Load orientation.** Read `VAULT_MANIFEST.md` at the vault root. This tells you:
- Where every directory is and what it's for
- Output routing rules (where to put your work)
- Conventions (frontmatter, linking, naming)
- Entry points (which MOC to start with)

**Step 2: Run silent health check:**

```bash
# 1. Check inbox
inbox_count=$(find inbox/ -maxdepth 1 -name '*.md' ! -name '_index.md' ! -name '_template.md' 2>/dev/null | wc -l)

# 2. Check for orphans in core dirs (sample — full scan only on explicit run)
orphan_sample=$(for f in concepts/*.md; do
  [ -f "$f" ] || continue
  name="${f##*/}"; name="${name%.md}"
  [ "$name" = "_template" ] && continue
  count=$(grep -rl "\[\[.*$name" concepts/ papers/ decisions/ projects/ 2>/dev/null | grep -v "$f" | wc -l)
  [ "$count" -eq 0 ] && echo "$f"
done | head -5)

# 3. Count thin notes (quick sample)
thin_count=$(find concepts/ papers/ decisions/ -name '*.md' ! -name '_template.md' -size -3k 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
```

**Step 3: Load task-relevant context.** If the user's first message implies a topic:
- Find the matching MOC (`concepts/MOC-*.md`) and skim it
- Check `lessons/` for past mistakes in that domain
- Check the relevant directory's `_index.md` for key notes

**Actions based on health check findings:**

| Finding | Action | User Interaction |
|---------|--------|------------------|
| Inbox has items | Report to user: "Inbox has N items. Want me to triage them?" | Ask once |
| Orphans detected | Auto-fix silently if < 5, report if >= 5 | Silent for small counts |
| Thin notes > 20 | Mention in first response: "N thin notes could use expansion" | Informational |
| All healthy | Say nothing — don't clutter with "all clear" messages | Silent |

### Agent Orientation Files

Every content and workflow directory has a `_index.md` file. Agents can read any directory's `_index.md` to understand:
- What goes in that directory
- Naming and frontmatter conventions
- Key notes to reference
- Related MOCs for deeper context

**Discovery pattern for any unfamiliar directory:**
```
Read <directory>/_index.md → understand purpose → follow routing rules
```

### Trigger: Hook Alert

The `vault-keeper-check.sh` PostToolUse hook runs after Write/Edit operations on `.md` files. When it detects issues, it prints an alert message. **When you see a vault-keeper alert in hook output, act on it:**

| Alert | Auto-Action |
|-------|-------------|
| `VAULT_KEEPER: inbox has N new items` | Offer to triage |
| `VAULT_KEEPER: new note has no inbound links` | Add 1-3 inbound wiki-links from relevant hub notes immediately |
| `VAULT_KEEPER: note missing frontmatter` | Add proper frontmatter to the note immediately |
| `VAULT_KEEPER: note has tags as string` | Convert tags to array immediately |

**Key rule:** For structural fixes (frontmatter, tags, missing links on new notes), fix immediately without asking. These are vault invariants, not preferences.

### Trigger: Bulk Operations

After any operation that creates or modifies 5+ vault notes (e.g., flesh-out runs, research imports, link campaigns):

1. Run a quick orphan check on affected files
2. Verify all new/modified notes have proper frontmatter
3. Confirm no broken links were introduced
4. Report metrics delta (links before → after)

### Trigger: User Context Clues

Proactively invoke vault keeper logic when the user's request implies vault maintenance, even without saying "vault keeper":

| User Says | Vault Keeper Interprets As |
|-----------|---------------------------|
| "Clean up the vault" | Full maintenance run |
| "What's in the inbox?" | Triage mode |
| "How healthy is the vault?" | Quick audit |
| "Connect these notes" | Link mode |
| "This note is thin" | Flesh-out mode |
| "Make this navigable" | MOC generation |
| "Update the maps" | MOC refresh |

### Proactive Rules

1. **Never announce proactive work unnecessarily.** If you silently fix 2 orphans, don't write a paragraph about it. A one-liner in your response is enough: "Also connected 2 orphan notes."
2. **Always fix invariant violations immediately.** Missing frontmatter, string tags, and orphaned new notes are bugs, not preferences.
3. **Respect context budget.** Proactive checks should cost < 500 tokens. If a full audit is needed, ask the user first.
4. **Don't repeat checks.** If you already ran a health check this session, don't run another unless new notes were created.
5. **Batch alerts.** If multiple issues are found, combine them into one report rather than alerting per-issue.

---

## Architecture

The Vault Keeper runs a **prioritized maintenance pipeline**. Each phase gates the next — critical issues are fixed before cosmetic ones.

```
Phase 1: TRIAGE     → Process inbox (new content enters the graph properly)
Phase 2: AUDIT      → Health check (identify what's broken)
Phase 3: HEAL       → Fix issues found by audit (frontmatter, broken links, orphans)
Phase 4: DENSIFY    → Expand thin notes, add missing connections
Phase 5: NAVIGATE   → Generate/update Maps of Content for traversability
Phase 6: REPORT     → Summary with metrics and deltas
```

---

## Phase 1: TRIAGE — Process Inbox

**Goal:** Zero inbox. New content enters the vault through proper channels.

```bash
ls inbox/*.md 2>/dev/null | grep -v '.base$'
```

If inbox has notes:
1. Invoke the **triage** skill logic for each note
2. Classify → research → structure → frontmatter → move → cross-link
3. Log what was triaged in the report

If inbox is empty, skip to Phase 2.

---

## Phase 2: AUDIT — Health Check

**Goal:** Identify all vault issues in a single pass.

Run these checks (parallel where possible):

### 2.1 Broken Wiki-Links
```bash
grep -roh '\[\[[^]]*\]\]' concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ lessons/ 2>/dev/null \
  | sed 's/\[\[//;s/\]\]//' | sed 's/|.*//' | sort -u | while read link; do
  found=0
  for d in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments projects lessons inbox daily Agents skills sessions missions retrospectives teleport docs meta specs benchmarks learnings research; do
    [ -f "$d/$link.md" ] && found=1 && break
  done
  [ $found -eq 0 ] && echo "$link"
done
```

### 2.2 Orphan Notes (No Inbound Links)
```bash
for dir in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments projects lessons; do
  for f in "$dir"/*.md; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    name="${f##*/}"; name="${name%.md}"
    [ "$name" = "_template" ] && continue
    count=$(grep -rl "\[\[.*$name" concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ Agents/ skills/ daily/ sessions/ missions/ retrospectives/ teleport/ docs/ lessons/ 2>/dev/null | grep -v "$f" | wc -l)
    [ "$count" -eq 0 ] && echo "ORPHAN: $f"
  done
done
```

### 2.3 Frontmatter Issues
```bash
for dir in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments projects lessons; do
  for f in "$dir"/*.md; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    head -1 "$f" | grep -q '^---' || echo "NO_FRONTMATTER: $f"
  done
done

# Tags as string instead of array
grep -rl '^tags:' concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ lessons/ 2>/dev/null | while read f; do
  grep '^tags:' "$f" | grep -qv '\[' && echo "TAGS_NOT_ARRAY: $f"
done
```

### 2.4 Thin Notes (Under 3KB)
```bash
for dir in concepts papers decisions patterns experiments lessons; do
  for f in "$dir"/*.md; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    b="${f##*/}"; [ "$b" = "_template.md" ] && continue
    sz=$(wc -c < "$f")
    [ "$sz" -lt 3000 ] && echo "THIN: $sz $f"
  done
done
```

### 2.5 Under-Connected Hubs (High Inbound, Low Outbound)
```bash
for f in concepts/*.md; do
  b="${f##*/}"; n="${b%.md}"; [ "$n" = "_template" ] && continue
  outbound=$(grep -oh '\[\[[^]]*\]\]' "$f" 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
  inbound=$(grep -rl "\[\[.*$n" concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ lessons/ 2>/dev/null | grep -v "$f" | wc -l)
  if [ "$inbound" -gt 15 ] && [ "$outbound" -lt 5 ]; then
    echo "UNDER-CONNECTED: $f (in:$inbound, out:$outbound)"
  fi
done
```

### 2.6 Missing Maps of Content
```bash
ls concepts/MOC-*.md 2>/dev/null | wc -l
# Target: at least 5 MOCs covering major topic clusters
```

Classify issues by severity:

| Severity | Issues |
|----------|--------|
| **CRITICAL** | Broken links in core concepts, orphan concepts, missing frontmatter |
| **HIGH** | Orphan papers/decisions/patterns, tags not arrays, under-connected hubs |
| **MEDIUM** | Thin notes (<3KB), missing MOCs |
| **LOW** | Thin lessons, cosmetic link improvements |

---

## Phase 3: HEAL — Fix Issues

**Goal:** Resolve all CRITICAL and HIGH issues automatically.

### 3.1 Fix Frontmatter
For each `NO_FRONTMATTER` or `TAGS_NOT_ARRAY` issue:
- Read the file
- Add/fix frontmatter with proper YAML (tags as arrays, title, date)
- Preserve all existing content

### 3.2 Fix Orphans
For each orphan note:
- Read the file to understand its topic
- Find 1-3 relevant hub notes
- Add inbound `[[wiki-links]]` from hub notes to the orphan

### 3.3 Fix Broken Links
For each broken link:
- Classify: is it a real missing note, a typo, or a code/template reference?
- Real missing note with 3+ references → create a stub concept note
- Typo → fix the link
- Code/template → ignore (non-actionable)

### 3.4 Fix Under-Connected Hubs
For each hub with high inbound but low outbound:
- Read the note
- Add 5-10 relevant outbound `[[wiki-links]]` to related notes

---

## Phase 4: DENSIFY — Expand and Connect

**Goal:** Increase content depth and graph density.

### 4.1 Flesh Out Thin Notes
Prioritize by inbound link count (highest-impact first):
1. Read the thin note
2. Research the topic (WebSearch for authoritative info)
3. Expand with: definition, key properties, examples, primary sources
4. Add outbound `[[wiki-links]]` (3-5 per note)
5. Add "Relevance to Cohezion" section if missing

**Budget:** Expand up to 15 notes per run (quality over quantity).

### 4.2 Add Missing Cross-Links
Run the **link** skill logic:
- Tag-based: notes sharing 2+ tags that don't link to each other
- Content-based: notes mentioning concepts that exist as vault notes
- Semantic proximity: notes in the same domain that should cross-reference

**Budget:** Add up to 50 bidirectional link pairs per run.

---

## Phase 5: NAVIGATE — Maps of Content

**Goal:** Make the vault traversable via curated entry points.

### 5.1 Identify Topic Clusters

Group notes by primary tag or domain:
```bash
# Find most common tags
grep -roh "tags: \[.*\]" concepts/ papers/ decisions/ patterns/ experiments/ projects/ lessons/ 2>/dev/null \
  | sed 's/tags: \[//;s/\]//' | tr ',' '\n' | sed 's/^ *//;s/ *$//' | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn | head -20
```

### 5.2 Generate/Update MOCs

For each major topic cluster (5+ notes), create or update a Map of Content:

```markdown
---
title: "MOC — [Topic Name]"
date: 2026-03-04
tags: [moc, navigation, [topic-tag]]
---

# Map of Content — [Topic Name]

## Overview
[1-2 sentences describing this topic area and its role in the vault]

## Core Concepts
- [[concept-1]] — [brief annotation from first sentence of note]
- [[concept-2]] — [annotation]

## Key Decisions
- [[decision-1]] — [annotation]

## Patterns
- [[pattern-1]] — [annotation]

## Research Papers
- [[paper-1]] — [annotation]

## Lessons Learned
- [[lesson-1]] — [annotation]

## Experiments
- [[experiment-1]] — [annotation]

## Entry Points
- Start here: [[most-linked-concept]]
- Deep dive: [[most-detailed-note]]
- Recent: [[most-recently-modified]]
```

**Target MOCs** (minimum set):
1. `MOC-agentic-ai.md` — Agent architecture, multi-agent systems, orchestration
2. `MOC-quantum-physics.md` — Quantum mechanics, computing, sensors, entanglement
3. `MOC-vault-architecture.md` — Knowledge graph, linking, vault health, conventions
4. `MOC-platform-infrastructure.md` — MCP, SurrealDB, Ollama, CI/CD, runbooks
5. `MOC-machine-learning.md` — ML, neural nets, training, optimization
6. `MOC-astrophysics.md` — Space, JWST, dark matter, exoplanets, gravitational waves
7. `MOC-compound-engineering.md` — Compound methodology, sessions, retrospectives
8. `MOC-safety-alignment.md` — AI safety, adversarial review, alignment, guardrails

### 5.3 Link MOCs to Each Other
Each MOC should link to 2-3 related MOCs in a "## Related Maps" section, creating a navigable top-level layer.

---

## Phase 6: REPORT — Summary

Generate a structured report:

```markdown
## Vault Keeper Report — YYYY-MM-DD

### Metrics
| Metric | Before | After | Delta |
|--------|--------|-------|-------|
| Total notes | X | Y | +Z |
| Wiki-links | X | Y | +Z |
| Orphan notes | X | Y | -Z |
| Broken links | X | Y | -Z |
| Frontmatter issues | X | Y | -Z |
| Thin notes (<3KB) | X | Y | -Z |
| Maps of Content | X | Y | +Z |
| Inbox items | X | Y | -Z |

### Actions Taken
- **Triaged:** X inbox notes processed
- **Healed:** X frontmatter fixes, Y orphans connected, Z broken links resolved
- **Densified:** X notes expanded, Y new cross-links added
- **Navigation:** X MOCs created/updated

### Remaining Issues
| Priority | Issue | Count |
|----------|-------|-------|
| MEDIUM | Thin notes | X |
| LOW | Cosmetic links | Y |

### Recommendations
1. [Top priority for next run]
2. [Second priority]
3. [Third priority]
```

---

## Quick Mode (`--quick`)

Skip Phases 1, 3, 4, 5. Only run Phase 2 (audit) and Phase 6 (report). Read-only — no modifications.

Useful for: daily check-ins, pre-session health verification, CI/CD integration.

## Context Mode (`--context "query"`)

Load relevant vault knowledge for a specific task:

1. Search vault for notes matching the query (by title, tags, content)
2. Load the most relevant MOC as a navigation entry point
3. Return top 5-10 notes with brief summaries
4. Include any relevant lessons (filtered by topic)

Output format:
```markdown
## Vault Context for: "[query]"

### Map of Content
→ [[MOC-relevant-topic]]

### Most Relevant Notes
1. [[note-1]] — [first sentence summary]
2. [[note-2]] — [summary]
...

### Applicable Lessons
- [[lesson-XX]] (severity: HIGH) — [title]
- [[lesson-YY]] (severity: MEDIUM) — [title]

### Related Decisions
- [[decision-1]] — [status: accepted]
```

This is the output that context hooks should inject at session startup.

---

## Scheduling

| Frequency | Mode | Purpose |
|-----------|------|---------|
| Every session start | `--context` | Load relevant knowledge |
| Daily | `--quick` | Health monitoring |
| Weekly | Full run | Comprehensive maintenance |
| After bulk imports | Full run | Integration and linking |
| After `/flesh-out` or `/link` runs | `--quick` | Verify improvements |

## Agent Deployment Strategy

For full maintenance runs, deploy specialist agents in parallel:

```
Agent 1: Triage (inbox processing)
Agent 2: Audit + Heal (health check and fixes)
Agent 3: Densify (flesh-out thin notes)
Agent 4: Densify (cross-linking)
Agent 5: Navigate (MOC generation)
```

This matches the proven pattern from vault densification sessions — 5 parallel agents with non-overlapping file scopes.

## Integration Points

### Claude Code Hooks
- **PreToolUse hook**: Run `--context` to load relevant vault knowledge before agent responds
- **PostToolUse hook**: Save session results back to vault (daily notes, new concepts)

### Cloud Vault MCP (port 8360)
- Use VaultOps for programmatic search
- Use ObsidianOps for frontmatter updates
- Use SurrealDB for graph queries

### Obsidian Graph View
- MOCs appear as high-connectivity hubs in the graph
- Color-code by directory type for visual navigation

## Quality Standards

**A healthy vault has:**
- 0 orphan notes in core directories
- 0 frontmatter issues
- 0 actionable broken links
- < 10% thin notes (<3KB) in any directory
- At least 5 Maps of Content
- Average 6+ wiki-links per note
- Every new note connected within 24 hours of creation

**The Vault Keeper's job is to maintain these invariants continuously.**

2. Key Technical Concepts:

   - **Compound engineering** — Simple, composable layers that each do one thing well and combine for emergent capability. Same infrastructure, new dimensions.
   - **PostToolUse hooks** — Shell scripts that run after tool operations on files, detecting issues and nudging behavior.
   - **Two-mode hook design** — Same vault-keeper-check.sh script serves Write/Edit (60s cooldown, per-file + vault-wide checks) and Read (300s cooldown, vault-wide only).
   - **AGENTS.md convention** — Vendor-neutral agent instruction file (Linux Foundation/AAIF standard). Read by OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Codex, Cursor, 20+ tools. Claude Code reads CLAUDE.md separately.
   - **Obsidian rich formats** — Callouts (`> [!tip]`), Mermaid diagrams (` ```mermaid`), LaTeX math (`$...Analysis:

), aliases (frontmatter), embeds (`![[note]]`), heading links (`[[note#heading]]`), block references (`^block-id`).
   - **Canvas files** — Obsidian `.canvas` JSON format with nodes (file, text, group), edges, colors. Companion to .md notes for spatial relationship visualization.
   - **SurrealDB 3.0** — Released Feb 2026. New features: SurrealMCP (official MCP server), Change Feeds, LIVE SELECT, Record References (bidirectional schema-level links), DEFINE API (custom REST endpoints), DEFINE EVENT (triggers), HNSW vector search, hybrid search (`search::rrf()`), computed fields, 8-22x faster graph queries.
   - **Cross-agent readiness** — Multiple AI tools (Claude Code, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Cursor, Windsurf, JetBrains) each read different instruction files. MCP is the universal integration layer.
   - **Vault-to-platform communication architecture** — Three-layer compound pattern: Hook → Change Journal (fast, non-blocking) → SurrealDB Sync (async, batched) → Agents via SurrealMCP.
   - **Authentication credential mismatch** — SurrealDB 3.0 instance runs with `sdb_admin_session43` credentials but all clients (cloud-vault-mcp, 3D graph plugin) default to `root:root`, making the entire pipeline operationally disconnected despite being structurally complete.

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/.claude/hooks/vault-keeper-check.sh`** (MODIFIED — multiple edits)
     - This is the core proactive mechanism. Modified to support two modes (Read vs Write/Edit), canvas nudges, callout nudges, and alias nudges.
     - Final state has 6 checks: inbox count, frontmatter, inbound links, canvas nudge, callout nudge, alias nudge.
     ```bash
     #!/usr/bin/env bash
     # PostToolUse hook: Proactive vault keeper checks on .md files.
     #
     # Two modes, same script:
     #   Write/Edit → per-file checks + vault-wide pulse (60s cooldown)
     #   Read       → vault-wide pulse only (5 min cooldown)
     #
     # Any agent touching vault .md files gets health awareness injected.
     # Alerts are prefixed with VAULT_KEEPER: so the agent can detect and act on them.

     VAULT_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)"

     # ── Extract tool info from stdin JSON ─────────────────────────────────────────
     stdin_json=$(cat)

     if ! command -v jq >/dev/null 2>&1; then
         exit 0
     fi

     tool_name=$(echo "$stdin_json" | jq -r '.tool_name // empty' 2>/dev/null)
     file_path=$(echo "$stdin_json" | jq -r '.tool_input.file_path // empty' 2>/dev/null)

     # Only run for .md files inside the vault
     [[ -z "$file_path" ]] && exit 0
     [[ "$file_path" != *.md ]] && exit 0
     [[ "$file_path" != "$VAULT_ROOT"* ]] && exit 0

     # Skip tooling directories
     [[ "$file_path" == *"/obsidian-plugin/"* ]] && exit 0
     [[ "$file_path" == *"/mcp-server/"* ]] && exit 0
     [[ "$file_path" == *"/tools/"* ]] && exit 0
     [[ "$file_path" == *"/.claude/"* ]] && exit 0

     # ── Mode-specific cooldown ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     if [[ "$tool_name" == "Read" ]]; then
         COOLDOWN_FILE="/tmp/vault-keeper-read-$(id -u).last"
         COOLDOWN_SECONDS=300
     else
         COOLDOWN_FILE="/tmp/vault-keeper-write-$(id -u).last"
         COOLDOWN_SECONDS=60
     fi

     if [[ -f "$COOLDOWN_FILE" ]]; then
         last_run=$(cat "$COOLDOWN_FILE" 2>/dev/null)
         now=$(date +%s)
         if [[ -n "$last_run" ]] && (( now - last_run < COOLDOWN_SECONDS )); then
             exit 0
         fi
     fi

     date +%s > "$COOLDOWN_FILE"

     # ── Vault-wide checks (both Read and Write/Edit) ─────────────────────────────
     alerts=""

     # 1. Inbox
     inbox_count=$(find "$VAULT_ROOT/inbox" -name '*.md' ! -name '.base' ! -name '_index.md' ! -name '_template.md' 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
     if [[ "$inbox_count" -gt 0 ]]; then
         alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: inbox has ${inbox_count} items waiting for triage\n"
     fi

     # ── Per-file checks (Write/Edit only) ────────────────────────────────────────
     if [[ "$tool_name" != "Read" && -f "$file_path" ]]; then
         rel_path="${file_path#$VAULT_ROOT/}"
         dir_name="${rel_path%%/*}"

         # 2. Frontmatter
         first_line=$(head -1 "$file_path" 2>/dev/null)
         case "$dir_name" in
             concepts|papers|decisions|patterns|experiments|projects|lessons)
                 if [[ "$first_line" != "---" ]]; then
                     alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: note missing frontmatter — ${rel_path}\n"
                 else
                     tags_line=$(grep '^tags:' "$file_path" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
                     if [[ -n "$tags_line" ]] && ! echo "$tags_line" | grep -q '\['; then
                         alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: note has tags as string (should be array) — ${rel_path}\n"
                     fi
                 fi
                 ;;
         esac

         # 3. Inbound links
         basename_md="${file_path##*/}"
         name="${basename_md%.md}"
         if [[ "$name" != "_template" && "$name" != "_index" && "$name" != "MOC-"* ]]; then
             inbound=$(grep -rl "\[\[.*${name}" "$VAULT_ROOT/concepts/" "$VAULT_ROOT/papers/" "$VAULT_ROOT/decisions/" "$VAULT_ROOT/patterns/" "$VAULT_ROOT/projects/" 2>/dev/null | grep -v "$file_path" | wc -l)
             if [[ "$inbound" -eq 0 ]]; then
                 alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: new note has no inbound links — ${rel_path}\n"
             fi
         fi

         # 4. Canvas nudge — high link density suggests visual mapping
         outbound=$(grep -coh '\[\[[^]]*\]\]' "$file_path" 2>/dev/null | awk '{s+=$1} END{print s+0}')
         canvas_path="${file_path%.md}.canvas"
         if [[ "$outbound" -ge 10 && ! -f "$canvas_path" ]]; then
             alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: note has ${outbound} outbound links — consider a companion .canvas to visualize relationships\n"
         fi

         # 5. Callout nudge — decisions and lessons benefit from structured callouts
         case "$dir_name" in
             decisions|lessons|patterns)
                 if ! grep -q '> \[!' "$file_path" 2>/dev/null; then
                     case "$dir_name" in
                         decisions) alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: decision has no callouts — consider > [!warning] for consequences, > [!tip] for rationale\n" ;;
                         lessons)   alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: lesson has no callouts — consider > [!danger] for the mistake, > [!success] for the fix\n" ;;
                         patterns)  alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: pattern has no callouts — consider > [!example] for code, > [!warning] for anti-patterns\n" ;;
                     esac
                 fi
                 ;;
         esac

         # 6. Alias nudge — concepts benefit from alternative names
         if [[ "$dir_name" == "concepts" && "$name" != "_template" && "$name" != "_index" && "$name" != "MOC-"* ]]; then
             if ! grep -q '^aliases:' "$file_path" 2>/dev/null; then
                 alerts="${alerts}VAULT_KEEPER: concept has no aliases — consider adding aliases: [\"alt name\"] for discoverability\n"
             fi
         fi
     fi

     # ── Output alerts ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     if [[ -n "$alerts" ]]; then
         echo -e "$alerts"
     fi

     exit 0
     ```

   - **`/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/.claude/settings.json`** (MODIFIED)
     - Added separate Read matcher for vault-keeper-check.sh (kept Write|Edit matcher for both vault-link-suggest.sh and vault-keeper-check.sh).
     ```json
     {
     ```

4. Errors and Fixes:

     - Problem: `.env` had no SurrealDB credentials. `config.py` defaults to `root:root`. SurrealDB runs with `sdb_admin_session43`.
     - Fix: Added correct credentials to `.env`. Systemd service already had correct credentials (discovered during investigation), so the running server was already working.
     - Verification: `curl` with `root:root` → "problem with authentication". `curl` with correct credentials → 102 papers returned. MCP `surrealdb_query` tool → working end-to-end.
   
   - **Health check false positive (identified, NOT yet fixed):**
     - Problem: `health.py` `check_surrealdb()` only hits unauthenticated `/health` endpoint, reports "connected: true" without testing actual query auth.
     - Status: Documented as E1-S1 in the PRD. Not yet implemented.
   
   - **3D graph plugin no auth (identified, NOT yet fixed):**
     - Problem: `SurrealDBClient.ts` sends queries to `/sql` without Authorization header or namespace/database headers.
     - Status: Documented as E1-S3 in the PRD. Not yet implemented.

   - **MCP endpoint discovery:**
     - Initial `POST /mcp` with `Accept: application/json` returned error: "Not Acceptable: Client must accept both application/json and text/event-stream"
     - Fix: Added `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream` header. Response came as SSE `data:` lines (30KB of tools).

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:

   The user's requests in this session formed two main phases:

   **Phase 1: Create a card documentation system** (from compaction instruction: "we need system cards, model cards, agent cards, embedding cards")
   - Design structured card templates for 4 card types inspired by industry standards
   - Create initial cards for all Cohezion infrastructure components, AI models, agents, and embeddings
   - Integrate cards into vault routing, cross-linking, and visual canvases
   - Research how major platforms (Anthropic, Google, OpenAI, HuggingFace) approach these documentation formats

   **Phase 2: Document all Gemini models** ("Document all the Gemini models")
   - Create model cards for every active (non-deprecated) Gemini model
   - Include pricing, benchmarks, capabilities, context windows, and Cohezion use cases

   - Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6 System Card PDF
   - Anthropic Claude Opus 4.6 System Card PDF
   - Anthropic Claude Haiku 4.5 System Card PDF
   - Google DeepMind Model Cards page
   - Instruction to "Research on the web with webmcp to see how other platforms are approaching these items"

2. Key Technical Concepts:

   - **System Cards** — Anthropic/OpenAI format (20-200+ pages): Introduction, Capabilities/Benchmarks, Safeguards, Alignment, Agentic Safety, RSP Evaluations. Adapted for Cohezion as infrastructure component documentation (services, databases, CLIs).
   - **Model Cards** — Google's 2018 "Model Cards for Model Reporting" (9 sections), HuggingFace extended template. Adapted for documenting AI models Cohezion uses with benchmarks, cost, safety, and Cohezion-specific use cases.
   - **Agent Cards** — Two emerging standards: (1) A2A Protocol (Google/Linux Foundation) — machine-readable JSON for agent interoperability at `/.well-known/agent.json`; (2) MICAI 2025 academic proposal for transparency/governance. NIST AI Agent Standards Initiative announced Feb 2026.
   - **Embedding Cards** — No formal standard; MTEB `ModelMeta` Pydantic schema is de facto. Key fields: `embed_dim`, `similarity_fn_name`, `max_tokens`, per-task MTEB scores.
   - **ASL-3 (AI Safety Level 3)** — Anthropic's safety classification for Claude Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6
   - **Google Frontier Safety Framework** — Google's equivalent; CCLs (Critical Capability Levels) tested but not breached
   - **Gemini model tiers** — Pro (frontier), Flash (mid-tier speed), Flash-Lite (economy), with generational progression (2.5 → 3 → 3.1)
   - **Benchmark suite** — SWE-bench Verified, Terminal-Bench 2.0, τ²-bench, MCP-Atlas, OSWorld, ARC-AGI-2, GPQA Diamond, MMMU-Pro, MMMLU, HLE
   - **MTEB (Massive Text Embedding Benchmark)** — Standard benchmark suite for embedding models

Frontmatter includes `card_type: model`, `provider`, `model_id`.

Frontmatter includes `card_type: agent`, `agent_type`.
